Fault Injection We inject four faulty types motivated by observed memor<sub>y</sub> risks (Sunil et al. 2026; Zou et al. 2025; H<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a</sub>li<sub>s</sub> <sub>e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.</sub> 2023<sub>;</sub> Ch<sub>ao</sub> <sub>e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.</sub> 2026<sub>;</sub> Ch<sub>en</sub> <sub>e</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>.</sub> 2025<sub>;</sub> Zh<sub>ang</sub> et al. 2026; Chen et al. 2024): poisoned records contain adversarial or corrupted values; stale records survive a failed deletion, update or consolidation; wrong-user records are associated with another user or context; and summary-drift corrupts consolidation output. A case is retained only if injection <sub>c</sub>h<sub>anges</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>answer an</sub>d <sub>crea</sub>t<sub>es a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t <sub>one</sub> d<sub>owns</sub>t<sub>ream</sub> <sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>us,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>recovery</sub> <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>er</sub> di<sub>ag-</sub> <sub>nose</sub>d f<sub>au</sub>lt<sub>s;</sub> it d<sub>oes</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>na</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l f<sub>au</sub>lt <sub>preva</sub>l<sub>ence</sub> <sub>or</sub> detector accuracy. <table><tr><td>Prompt</td><td>Key instruction summary</td><td>Output fields</td></tr><tr><td>Claim generation</td><td>Produce a concise claim for the user&#x27;s current intent from the request, retrieved Claim; cited evidence memories, and prior turns; cite all supporting memory, prior turn, and current input ids.</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Tool planning</td><td>Plan only valid tool calls. Use read-only tools for external or volatile information Tool steps; tool name; argu- and side-effecting tools only under an explicit current instruction. Ground tool ments; cited evidence arguments in available evidence, avoid invented ids, repeated failed calls, and duplicates.</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Response generation</td><td>Answer from the claim, memories, and tool results. The response must be Response; cited evidence grounded, concise, and faithful to observed tool outcomes: it must not invent unavailable data, ignore returned results, or report a side-effecting action as completed unless this turn&#x27;s tool result supports it.</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Memory mutation</td><td>Propose durable memory writes, updates, deletes, or consolidations. Memories New memories; updates; encode atomic facts; tool observation memories are immutable; every operation deletes; consolidations; prove- records provenance so later repair can identify the memories and trace steps nance supporting each mutation.</td><td></td></tr></table>

<table><tr><td>Prompt</td><td>Key instruction summary</td><td>Output fields</td></tr><tr><td>Missing action replan</td><td>Reuse the planning contract over the repaired active state to decide whether additional tool calls are still required; empty plans are valid and tool names must come from the available tool set.</td><td>Tool steps: cited evidence</td></tr><tr><td>Claim and answer replay</td><td>Regenerate the claim or final answer from repaired memories, repaired history, Claim or response; cited evi- and repaired tool observations, citing only evidence visible in the repaired dence context.</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Targeted tool action replay</td><td>Re-derive one replacement call for each superseded tool action. Old calls Target action id; tool name; ar- and observations are untrusted hints; replacements may change tool name or guments; cited evidence arguments when needed, but may not introduce extra unbound calls.</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Targeted memory replay</td><td>Re-derive one replacement for each superseded memory mutation step usingTarget step id; content; source; repaired evidence while preserving the target semantic type and avoiding trust score; cited evidence protected preserved memory outputs.</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>LLM-judge repair baseline</td><td>Predict a rollback plan from compact faulty artifacts, including faulty memo- Delete; quarantine; invalidate; ries, faulty trace steps, session turns, the wrong final answer, valid ids, and the replay; preserve; redundant; executor plan contract. The prompt requires strict JSON, forbids invented ids, suspicious; reason deletes source faulty memories, and requests a minimal dependency complete replay region.</td><td></td></tr></table>

Table 13: Prompt contracts for rollback replay and the LLM-judge repair baseline.

E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>man</sub>if<sub>es</sub>t <sub>row con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns a</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k id<sub>en</sub>tifi<sub>er an</sub>d <sub>an or</sub>d<sub>ere</sub>d li<sub>s</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f f<sub>au</sub>lt <sub>spec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>ons:</sub>

$$
r = ( \tau , [ f _ { 1 } , \dots , f _ { k } ] ) .
$$

Here, τ denotes the task identifier, and faults are applied sequentially in the listed order. Each fault specification f<sub>i</sub> <sub>recor</sub>d<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>au</sub>lt t<sub>ype</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>any</sub> f<sub>au</sub>lt <sub>spec</sub>ifi<sub>c</sub> fi<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>s</sub> <sub>nee</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> injection, such as the target memory, target mutation step, corru<sub>p</sub>te<sup>d</sup> content, or wron<sub>g</sub>-user content. <sup>C</sup>orru<sub>p</sub>te<sup>d</sup> content i<sub>s use</sub>d f<sub>or man</sub>if<sub>es</sub>t <sub>spec</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d <sub>corrup</sub>ti<sub>ons, w</sub>hil<sub>e wrong-user</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t i<sub>s use</sub>d <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>or wrong-user</sub> f<sub>au</sub>lt<sub>s.</sub>

Poisoned. For poisoned memory faults, the manifest names a target memory produced by a memory write step. <table><tr><td>Condition Rollback rule</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Diagnosed faulty memory</td><td>Delete it from the active memory store.</td></tr><tr><td>Unsupported affected memory</td><td>Quarantine it and exclude it from subsequent retrieval.</td></tr><tr><td>Candidate supported by independent evidence</td><td>Preserve the existing trace output, together with any unchanged downstream action or observation that depends on it.</td></tr><tr><td>Unsupported answer-relevant execution node</td><td>Invalidate the old output and replay the corresponding executable step.</td></tr><tr><td>Unsupported non-answer-relevant execution node Diagnosed faulty-memory provenance seed</td><td>Invalidate the old output without replay and exclude it from subsequent context.</td></tr><tr><td></td><td>If a provenance seed exists, replay the step that produced the faulty memory to generate a corrected replacement.</td></tr><tr><td>Final-answer node</td><td>Always regenerate the final answer under the repaired memory and trace state.</td></tr></table>

Table 14: Rule-guided rollback decisions. Memory dispositions determine which records remain active, whereas trace decisions d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>ne w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>or ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>s are preserve</sub>d<sub>,</sub> i<sub>nva</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>, or rep</sub>l<sub>aye</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

$$
\mathrm { R e c o v e r y } = \frac { N _ { \mathrm { s u c c } } } { N _ { \mathrm { s u c c } } + N _ { \mathrm { f a i l } } } .

For candidate memory m, the anomaly score is

$$
\operatorname { a n o m a l y } ( m ) = { \frac { \operatorname { c o n t r a d i c t i o n } ( m ) } { \operatorname { s u p p o r t } ( m ) + \operatorname { c o n t r a d i c t i o n } ( m ) } } ,
$$

with score 0 when the denominator is zero. In the oracle-<sub>ass</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d di<sub>agnos</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>,</sub> if b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k <sub>prov</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>au</sub>lt<sub>y</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>memory,</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>memory</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>ass</sub>i<sub>gne</sub>d anomaly score 1.0. Counterfactual influence is computed by d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> $m ,$ <sub>rerunn</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>user</sub> t<sub>urn, an</sub>d <sub>scor</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>regenera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>race w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>orac</sub>l<sub>e:</sub>

<table><tr><td>Method</td><td>Repair level</td><td>Memory repair</td><td>Trace rollback</td><td>Dependency graph</td></tr><tr><td>No repair</td><td>None</td><td>No</td><td>No</td><td>No</td></tr><tr><td>Full memory reset</td><td>Memory</td><td>All memories</td><td>No</td><td>No</td></tr><tr><td>Delete retrieved memories</td><td>Memory</td><td>Retrieved memories</td><td>No</td><td>No</td></tr><tr><td>MemAudit-style</td><td>Memory</td><td>Ranked memories</td><td>No</td><td>Memory only</td></tr><tr><td>LLM-judge repair</td><td>Trace</td><td>LLM-selected</td><td>LLM-selected</td><td>No</td></tr><tr><td>AgentTrace-style</td><td>Trace</td><td>Indirect</td><td>Suspicious steps</td><td>Trace only</td></tr><tr><td>Ours</td><td>Memory+trace</td><td>Selective</td><td>Selective</td><td>Memory+trace</td></tr></table>

Table 20: Comparison of repair baselines. Baselines difer in whether they repair memory state, roll back faulty trace state, or use <sup>d</sup>e<sub>p</sub>en<sup>d</sup>enc<sub>y</sub> structure.

$$
\mathrm { i n f l u e n c e } ( m ) = \frac { \operatorname* { m a x } ( 0 , S _ { - m } - S _ { \mathrm { o r i g } } ) } { 1 - S _ { \mathrm { o r i g } } } ,
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $S _ { \mathrm { o r i g } }$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l f<sub>au</sub>lt<sub>y</sub> t<sub>race score an</sub>d $S _ { - m }$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> score after deleting m. If the original score is already 1, or if the rerun fails, the influence score is set to 0. The final score i<sub>s</sub>

$$
\operatorname { s c o r e } ( m ) = \alpha \cdot \operatorname { i n f l u e n c e } ( m ) + ( 1 - \alpha ) \cdot \operatorname { a n o m a l y } ( m ) ,
$$

<sub>w</sub>ith $\alpha = 0 . 5$ . Memories with score above 0.5 are deleted t<sub>oge</sub>th<sub>er, an</sub>d <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>user</sub> t<sub>urn</sub> i<sub>s rep</sub>l<sub>aye</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> b<sub>ase-</sub> li<sub>ne</sub> d<sub>oes no</sub>t <sub>ro</sub>ll b<sub>ac</sub>k t<sub>race no</sub>d<sub>es,</sub> i<sub>nva</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ms, repa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>memory</sub> li<sub>neage,</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t <sub>answer</sub> <sub>re</sub>l<sub>evan</sub>t <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ay</sub> f<sub>ron</sub>ti<sub>ers.</sub>

C<sub>an</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>eps are res</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o memory rea</sub>d<sub>, c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>m, p</sub>l<sub>an,</sub> t<sub>oo</sub>l <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on, an</sub>d fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>answer s</sub>t<sub>eps</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>are</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>war</sub>d <sub>reac</sub>h<sub>-</sub> <sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e erroneous</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>answer an</sub>d f<sub>au</sub>lt<sub>-re</sub>l<sub>evan</sub>t<sub>.</sub> A <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ep</sub> i<sub>s</sub> f<sub>au</sub>lt<sub>-re</sub>l<sub>evan</sub>t if it li<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e a</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>race reg</sub>i<sub>on,</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y uses</sub> f<sub>au</sub>lt<sub>y memory,</sub> b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ongs</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>au</sub>lt d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t sufix, or is the final answer step. Each candidate v receives

$$
